2. Marginal lending facility (Title II, Chapter 1)
																URL:       https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/guideline/2015/510/2025-06-16/eng
																LOCATION:  Art. 18(1)-(4)
																QUOTE:     "Counterparties may use the marginal lending facility to obtain overnight liquidity from the Eurosystem through a reverse transaction with their home NCB at a pre-specified interest rate using eligible assets as collateral." ... 																NAMES:     AGENCY ONLY

																QUOTE:     "Institutions fulfilling the eligibility criteria under Article 55 and which have access to an account with the NCB where the transaction can be settled, notably in TARGET, may access the marginal lending facility." / "Access to the marginal lending facility shall be granted only on TARGET business days with the exclusion of the days on which TARGET is not available at the end of the day due to a 'prolonged TARGET disruption over several business days' as referred to in Article 187a."

																NAMES:     Guideline (EU) 2022/912 (ECB/2022/8) — Appendix V to Annex I (TARGET Guideline)

																URL:       https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/guideline/2015/510/2025-06-16/eng

6. Climate-factor provisions — candidate, flagged not confirmed
																URL:       https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/guideline/2015/510/2025-06-16/eng
																LOCATION:  Art. 63(1)(c)
																QUOTE:     "multi-step or floating coupons with steps linked to SPTs, provided that: (i) the compliance with SPTs by the issuer, or any undertaking belonging to the same sustainability-linked bond issuer group, is subject to verification by an independent third party in accordance with the terms and conditions of the debt instrument;"
																NAMES:     AGENCY ONLY (references "SPT" — sustainability performance target — defined at Art. 2(88a))
																TENSE:     PRESENT
																NOTES:     Art. 2(88a) defines SPT with reference to "Regulation (EU) 2020/852" (environmental objectives) and UN Sustainable Development Goals "relating to climate change or environmental degradation." This is an ELIGIBILITY provision for sustainability-linked bond coupon structures — NOT a haircut/risk-control climate-factor adjustment. The G.09 edge case "Climate-factor adjustments (referenced but not yet fully extracted)" most likely points to the haircut schedule in Guideline (EU) 2016/65 (already opened), not to 2015/510. Recommend the next agent verify against 2016/65's own text rather than treat this SPT provision as satisfying that edge case. NOT FOUND: no direct climate-related valuation-haircut adjustment provision within 2015/510 itself — searched strings "climate", "environmental", "sustainab*" within the fetched Parts One through Four (Title I) of the consolidated text.

Stop point reached. No second Member-State NSI or municipal material was touched. Handing over per G.09 §5 (report and stop).

																NOTES:     Exact rate parameter — usable directly in a financial model. Capped-base rule (50% of GNI) is a second parameter, also directly usable.
																FILE:      SEC03.pdf
																LOCATION:  Article 1 4 0 remarks — GNI own resource
																QUOTE:     "The rate to be applied to the Member States' GNI for financial year 2027 is 0,7495 %."
																NAMES:     GNI own resource, call rate
																TENSE:     PRESENT
																NOTES:     GNI-based resource is explicitly the balancing item ("ensures that the budget is always balanced ex ante") — its rate is a residual, not an independently set parameter; a financial model treating it as an input rather than a derived output would misrepresent the mechanism.
																FILE:      SEC03.pdf
																LOCATION:  Article 1 7 0 remarks — plastic-packaging own resource
																QUOTE:     "The uniform call rate shall be EUR 0,80 per kilogram. Certain Member States shall be entitled to annual lump-sum reductions."
																NAMES:     Non-recycled plastic packaging waste own resource
																TENSE:     PRESENT
																NOTES:     Per-kilogram rate, not a percentage — different unit from VAT/GNI resources; keep units explicit if these all land in the same model table.
																FILE:      SEC03.pdf
																LOCATION:  Title 1 own-resources tables — per-Member-State breakdown
																QUOTE:     Full 27-Member-State breakdown given for each own-resource line (Customs duties, VAT, GNI, plastic, GNI lump-sum reductions) for 2027/2026/2025.
																NAMES:     all 27 EU Member States
																TENSE:     PRESENT
																NOTES:     Not reproduced line-by-line here (27 rows × 4 resource types = 108 data points) — figures are in the source PDF and were read; available on request if the financial model needs per-country granularity rather than EU-aggregate figures. GNI lump-sum reductions table shows several Member States (Germany, Netherlands, Sweden, Austria, Denmark) with negative figures — i.e. they pay MORE, not less, under this mechanism; the "reduction" nets against payers.

NOT YET EXTRACTED (flagged, not fabricated)
SEC03 pages ~16–1,114: Titles 2–6 of Commission revenue (surpluses/balances, administrative revenue, financial revenue, budgetary guarantees, policy-related revenue), and the ENTIRE Commission expenditure side (this is where the bulk of the EU's ~€200bn in spending actually lives — Horizon Europe, cohesion funds, CAP, external action, etc.). SEC00 gave EU-wide MFF-heading totals and some programme-level detail (Horizon Europe clusters, InvestEU, CEF) in its narrative sections, which is a partial substitute but not the same as SEC03's own itemised Title/Chapter/Article structure.
SEC03 Title 2: "SURPLUSES, BALANCES AND ADJUSTMENTS" — p.m. for 2027 estimate per the revenue summary table, so likely low future-extraction value, but not confirmed empty in detail.
Part A — SEC03 expenditure extraction (top-down, batch 1)
Batch ID: SEC03-EXP-2026-08-03-A1 Strand: Finances / EU institutional budget — Section III (Commission) expenditure Governing briefs: Research.2 v2.1 (fixed) + Research.EU v0.1 (fixed) + Research.3 (amendment) Source document: SEC03.pdf — "SECTION III — COMMISSION", Draft General Budget of the European Union for the financial year 2027. 1,114 pages, A4, PDF creation date 2026-07-08. Scope of this batch: Expenditure master summary (all 20 Titles), TITLE 01 chapter summary, CHAPTER 01 01 structure, CHAPTER 01 02 remarks + legal basis. Presentation rule: field format, one entry per table row / per provision, full parameter values, no adjudication.

Layer 0 — Expenditure master summary, by Title
Printed pages 72–73. Table header reads: "EXPENDITURE / Figures / Title / Heading / 2027 appropriations (Commitments, Payments) / 2026 appropriations (Commitments, Payments) / 2025 out-turn (Commitments, Payments)".
Figures are in EUR. 2027 and 2026 columns are whole euro; 2025 out-turn carries decimal centimes.

E-000 — table header / column definition
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Expenditure summary table, column header row, printed p. 72 QUOTE: "2027 appropriations / 2026 appropriations / 2025 out-turn — Commitments / Payments" NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED TENSE: N/A — column header, no relationship stated NOTES: Establishes the three-column-pair structure used throughout the expenditure section. The commitments/payments distinction is the structural basis for every entry below. 2025 figures are labelled "out-turn" (actuals), 2026 and 2027 as "appropriations" — a stated distinction in kind, not just in year.

Layer 2 — CHAPTER 01 01 structure and CHAPTER 01 02 legal basis

C0101-001 — Chapter 01 01 legal-basis pointer
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Chapter 01 01, "Legal basis" block, printed p. 74 QUOTE: "Legal basis / See Chapter 01 02." NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED — cross-reference only TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: The support-administrative chapter states no legal basis of its own and points to the operational chapter. This is a documented internal cross-reference, not a named instrument. Whether the same pattern holds for the other 19 Titles' 01-chapters is not yet checked.

C0101-002 — Article 01 01 01 subtotal
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Chapter 01 01 table, "Article 01 01 01 — Subtotal" row, printed p. 74 QUOTE: "Article 01 01 01 — Subtotal 861 204 182 837 906 671 802 524 004,62 93,19 %" NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED TENSE: N/A — figures row NOTES: Support expenditure for Horizon Europe. 2027 861 204 182; 2026 837 906 671; 2025 out-turn 802 524 004,62. This table carries an extra column absent from the summary tables — a 2025/2027 execution ratio, here 93,19 %. That ratio column is a derived figure printed in the source; it is quoted as printed and not recomputed. The 861 204 182 figure matches the "Support expenditure for ‘Horizon Europe’ 01 01 01" line reported in SEC(2026) 250 as EUR 861,2 million — the two documents agree at this line.

C0101-003 — Item 01 01 01 01
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Chapter 01 01 table, Item 01 01 01 01 row, printed p. 73 QUOTE: "01 01 01 01 Expenditure related to officials and temporary staff implementing Horizon Europe — Indirect research 1.0.11 188 512 961 187 011 774 167 019 148,93 88,60 %" NAMES: Horizon Europe TENSE: N/A — figures row NOTES: Carries an MFF sub-heading code, 1.0.11 — a column present in the article/item tables but not in the Title- or Chapter-level summaries. This code is the mechanical link from each budget line to an MFF heading, and is the strongest structural material found so far in this document for tying budget lines to the MFF framework. Every item row in Chapter 01 01 carries one.

C0101-004 — Item 01 01 01 01 remarks
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 01, "Remarks" block, printed p. 75 QUOTE: "This appropriation is intended to cover expenditure relating to officials and temporary staff implementing the specific research and innovation programme — Horizon Europe and occupying posts on the authorised establishment plans engaged in indirect research actions, including officials and temporary staff posted in Union delegations." NAMES: Horizon Europe TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: "This appropriation is intended to..." is the standard opening formula for every Remarks block in this document. Names the establishment plan as the authorising instrument for the posts — the establishment plan is published within the budget itself, so it is an internal reference rather than an external terminus.

C0101-005 — Item 01 01 01 01 assigned revenue
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 01, "Assigned revenue" block, printed pp. 75–76 QUOTE: "Assigned revenue (origin, estimated amounts and corresponding article or item of the statement of revenue). / EFTA-EEA 4 939 040 6 6 0 0 / Candidate countries and Western Balkan potential candidates 509 823 6 0 1 0" NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: This is a documented internal cross-reference structure worth noting for the graph: each assigned-revenue line names the revenue article or item that feeds it (6 6 0 0, 6 0 1 0) — i.e. expenditure lines in this document explicitly point back to revenue lines in Titles 6 and 6 0 of the same document. That is a stated input relationship between two parts of the same publication, running expenditure ← revenue. Whether the project treats intra-document line references as edges is a scoping question for the other end of the process; recorded here so the option exists.

FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Chapter 01 02, "Legal basis" block, first cited act, printed p. 90 QUOTE: "Council Regulation (EU) 2020/2094 of 14 December 2020 establishing a European Union Recovery Instrument to support the recovery in the aftermath of the COVID-19 crisis (OJ L 433 I, 22.12.2020, p. 23, ELI: http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg/2020/2094/oj)." Same instrument already appears in the Soft Connections corpus via NGEU material.

HZ-002 — Chapter 01 02 legal basis, act 2 of 5
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Chapter 01 02, "Legal basis" block, second cited act, printed p. 90 QUOTE: "Regulation (EU) 2021/695 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 28 April 2021 establishing Horizon Europe – the Framework Programme for Research and Innovation, laying down its rules for participation and dissemination, and repealing Regulations (EU) No 1290/2013 and (EU) No 1291/2013 (OJ L 170, 12.5.2021, p. 1, ELI: http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg/2021/695/oj)." Also names two repealed predecessor regulations — repeal is a documented relationship of a different kind from dependency; recorded as stated, not classified.

HZ-004 — Chapter 01 02 legal basis, act 4 of 5
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Chapter 01 02, "Legal basis" block, fourth cited act, printed p. 91 QUOTE: "Regulation (EU) 2021/947 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 9 June 2021 establishing the Neighbourhood, Development and International Cooperation Instrument – Global Europe, amending and repealing Decision No 466/2014/EU and repealing Regulation (EU) 2017/1601 and Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 480/2009 (OJ L 209, 14.6.2021, p. 1, ELI: http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg/2021/947/oj)." NAMES: Regulation (EU) 2021/947; Decision No 466/2014/EU; Regulation (EU) 2017/1601; Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 480/2009 TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Odd — flagged, not resolved. The Global Europe / NDICI regulation is cited as a legal basis for the Horizon Europe chapter. The chapter Remarks do not explain the link, and the same instrument governs Title 14 (External Action). HZ-005 — Chapter 01 02 legal basis, act 5 of 5
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Chapter 01 02, "Legal basis" block, fifth cited act, printed p. 91 QUOTE: "Regulation (EU) 2021/2115 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 2 December 2021 establishing rules on support for strategic plans to be drawn up by Member States under the common agricultural policy (CAP Strategic Plans) and financed by the European Agricultural Guarantee Fund (EAGF) and by the European Agricultural Fund for Rural Development (EAFRD) and repealing Regulations (EU) No 1305/2013 (EU) No 1307/2013 (OJ L 435, 6.12.2021, p. 1, ELI: http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg/2021/2115/oj)." NAMES: Regulation (EU) 2021/2115; European Agricultural Guarantee Fund (EAGF); European Agricultural Fund for Rural Development (EAFRD); Regulation (EU) No 1305/2013; Regulation (EU) No 1307/2013 TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Same oddity as HZ-004, flagged not resolved. The CAP Strategic Plans regulation is cited as a legal basis for the Horizon Europe chapter. Not explained in the chapter Remarks. Two of the five acts cited as legal basis for Horizon Europe are instruments whose primary subject is another Title entirely (14 External Action, 08 Agriculture). The pattern is worth checking against other chapters before anything is made of it.

HZ-006 — Chapter 01 02 remarks, NGEU assigned revenue
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Chapter 01 02, "Remarks" block, penultimate paragraph, printed p. 90 QUOTE: "In addition, and in accordance with Regulation (EU) 2020/2094, external assigned revenue resulting from proceeds of the European Union Recovery Instrument entered in the statement of revenue give rise to the provision of appropriations for this programme under this title for a total amount of EUR 5 412 000 000 in commitments." NAMES: Regulation (EU) 2020/2094; European Union Recovery Instrument TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: States a full parameter value — EUR 5 412 000 000 in commitments — and a direct input relationship: revenue entered in the statement of revenue gives rise to appropriations under this title. The sentence continues: "Such amounts had to be legally committed before the end of 2023 with the exception of administrative expenditure for which the amounts are indicated in the budget remarks of the relevant budget lines under this title." That continuation is PAST tense as to the commitment deadline, while the first sentence is present tense as to the mechanism — the two tenses sit in adjacent sentences of the same block. Flagged per Research.2 §5b.

HZ-007 — Chapter 01 02 remarks, third-country contributions
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Chapter 01 02, "Remarks" block, printed p. 90 QUOTE: "In accordance with Articles 21, 22 and 24 of the Financial Regulation, contributions received from third countries (EFTA States pursuant to the Agreement on the European Economic Area, candidate countries and, if applicable, the Western Balkan potential candidates, or other third countries) for participating in Union programmes and any other assigned revenue entered in the statement of revenue give rise to the provision of corresponding appropriations and to implementation under this chapter." NAMES: Financial Regulation; Agreement on the European Economic Area TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Named at "the Financial Regulation" with article numbers (21, 22, 24) but without its citation number at this location — Regulation (EU, Euratom) 2024/2509 is given elsewhere in the SEC(2026) 250 document but is not restated here. Records the mechanism behind the EFTA-EEA and candidate-country assigned-revenue lines extracted at C0101-005.

Open discrepancies recorded, not resolved:

Structural findings for the remaining extraction:
Article/item tables carry an MFF sub-heading code column (e.g. 1.0.11) absent from Title/Chapter summaries. This is the mechanical budget-line → MFF-heading link.
Article/item tables carry a 2025/2027 execution-ratio column as a derived figure printed in the source.
"Legal basis" blocks appear 671 times in the document. These are the highest-value edge material in SEC03 — each is a named legal act with OJ reference and ELI.
Support-administrative chapters (X 01) may systematically defer their legal basis to the operational chapter ("See Chapter 01 02"). Confirmed for Title 01 only.
p.m. entries are token lines, not zeros, and appear throughout.
Assigned-revenue blocks under expenditure items name the revenue article/item that feeds them, creating stated intra-document expenditure ← revenue references.
Remaining in SEC03, top-down queue:
Title 01: Chapter 01 01 remaining articles/items (01 01 02 onward); Chapter 01 02 articles 01 02 01 to 01 02 99 and their items; Chapters 01 03, 01 04, 01 20.
Titles 02 through 16: full chapter/article/item extraction. Title 08 (Agriculture, 54,9bn CA) and Title 05 (Regional Development, 44,0bn CA) are the two largest and will be the two longest.
Titles 20, 21, 30: administrative expenditure, European Schools and Pensions, Reserves.
Sections after p. 34720 of the text layer: annexed Offices (Publications Office, EPSO, PMO, OIB and others) each carrying their own revenue and administrative expenditure titles — a distinct sub-document not covered by the Title 01–30 structure and not yet scoped.
Revenue Titles 3, 4, 6 (Titles 1 and the headline revenue table already done per G.13).

CORR-001 — entry C0101-001 is mislocated
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Article 01 01 01, "Legal basis" block, printed p. 75 QUOTE: "Legal basis / See Chapter 01 02." NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED — cross-reference only TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Batch 1 recorded this as "Chapter 01 01, 'Legal basis' block, printed p. 74". Both parts are wrong. C0101-032 — Remarks
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 02, "Remarks" block, printed p. 76 QUOTE: "This appropriation is intended to cover expenditure on external personnel implementing the specific research and innovation programme — Horizon Europe in the form of indirect actions, including external personnel posted in Union delegations and the salary and other associated costs of the President of the European Research Council." NAMES: Horizon Europe; European Research Council TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Names a single office-holder's remuneration (the President of the European Research Council) as a component of a budget item. This is the only named individual office in Chapter 01 01.
C0101-033 — Assigned revenue
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 02, "Assigned revenue" block, printed p. 76 QUOTE: "Proceeds from EURI 2 645 319 5 0 4 0 / EFTA-EEA 1 461 867 6 6 0 0 / Candidate countries and Western Balkan potential candidates 153 232 6 0 1 0 / Other countries 16 060 054 6 0 1 0" NAMES: EURI (European Union Recovery Instrument) TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: First appearance in the extraction of revenue code 5 0 4 0 ("Proceeds from EURI"), a revenue item in Title 5, distinct from the Title 6 codes (6 6 0 0, 6 0 1 0) seen so far. The stated intra-document reference therefore runs to two different revenue Titles from a single expenditure item.
Item 01 01 01 03 — Other management expenditure for Horizon Europe — Indirect research
C0101-034 — Remarks
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 03, "Remarks" block, printed p. 76 QUOTE: "This appropriation is intended to cover other administrative expenditure for the management of the specific research and innovation programme — Horizon Europe, in the form of indirect actions, including other administrative expenditure incurred by staff posted in Union delegations." NAMES: Horizon Europe TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Opening paragraph of a four-paragraph block. The remaining three paragraphs enumerate covered expenditure categories (technical and administrative assistance; IT systems; building-related expenditure) and name no publications.
C0101-035 — Remarks, IT systems paragraph
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 03, "Remarks" block, third paragraph, printed p. 76 QUOTE: "It is also intended to cover expenditure related to the development and maintenance of IT systems needed for the management and implementation of the programme." NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Quoted because the IT-systems formula recurs across items in this chapter with varying wording (compare Item 01 01 02 03, which folds IT systems and building expenditure into one sentence and adds "corporate IT systems"). The variation is in the source, not introduced here.

Item 01 01 01 11 — Expenditure related to officials and temporary staff implementing Horizon Europe — Direct research
C0101-037 — Remarks, JRC establishment plan and action types
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 11, "Remarks" block, first paragraph and indents, printed p. 77 QUOTE: "This appropriation is intended to cover expenditure relating to officials and temporary staff occupying posts on the authorised establishment plan of the Joint Research Centre (JRC) and implementing the specific research and innovation programme — Horizon Europe, and in particular: — direct action, consisting of research activities, scientific and technical support activities, and exploratory research activities undertaken in the establishments of the JRC and in Union delegations, — indirect action, consisting of programmes implemented as part of the JRC's activities conducted on a competitive basis." NAMES: Joint Research Centre (JRC); Horizon Europe TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Defines the direct/indirect action split for the JRC. The "authorised establishment plan" is published within this same budget document (Annex 'Staff' to this section), so it is an internal reference rather than an external terminus.
C0101-038 — Remarks, competitive activities
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 11, "Remarks" block, penultimate paragraph and indents, printed p. 77 QUOTE: "This appropriation can be supplemented by appropriations that the JRC will receive by participating on a competitive basis in indirect actions and in scientific and technical activities in support of Union policies. The activities of a competitive nature conducted by the JRC shall consist of: — activities carried out following grant or procurement procedures, — activities on behalf of third parties, — activities undertaken under an administrative agreement with other institutions or other Commission departments for the provision of technical-scientific services." NAMES: Joint Research Centre (JRC) TENSE: PRESENT / FUTURE ("will receive") NOTES: This paragraph is repeated verbatim in Items 01 01 01 12, 01 01 01 13, 01 01 02 11, 01 01 02 12 and 01 01 02 13 — six occurrences in this chapter. It names a revenue stream ("activities on behalf of third parties", "administrative agreement with other institutions") that is not itself a publication and is not given a revenue code at these locations. Candidate unpublishable terminus material under Research.2 §4; not classified here.

Item 01 01 01 71 — European Research Council Executive Agency — Contribution from Horizon Europe
C0101-045 — Remarks
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 71, "Remarks" block, printed p. 79 QUOTE: "This appropriation is intended to cover the operating costs of the European Research Council Executive Agency incurred as a result of the Executive Agency's role in the implementation of the specific research and innovation programme — Horizon Europe (2021-2027) and the completion of its predecessor programmes. The establishment plan of the Executive Agency is set out in Annex 'Staff' to this section." NAMES: European Research Council Executive Agency; Horizon Europe; Annex 'Staff' to this section TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: This two-sentence formula is identical for all five executive-agency items in this article, varying only in the agency name. It names an internal document — Annex 'Staff' to Section III — as the source of the agency's establishment plan. That annex is published within this same budget document and has not yet been located or extracted; it is one of the sections after the Title 01–30 structure flagged in batch 1.

LB-71-01 — Legal basis, act 1 of 7
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 71, "Legal basis" block, first cited act, printed p. 79 QUOTE: "Council Regulation (EC) No 58/2003 of 19 December 2002 laying down the statute for executive agencies to be entrusted with certain tasks in the management of Community programmes (OJ L 11, 16.1.2003, p. 1, ELI: http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg/2003/58/oj)." NAMES: Council Regulation (EC) No 58/2003 TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: The constitutive act for executive agencies generally. Cited first in all five executive-agency legal-basis blocks in this article.
LB-71-02 — Legal basis, act 2 of 7
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 71, "Legal basis" block, second cited act, printed p. 79 QUOTE: "Regulation (EC) No 1906/2006 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 18 December 2006 laying down the rules for the participation of undertakings, research centres and universities in actions under the Seventh Framework Programme and for the dissemination of research results (2007-2013) (OJ L 391, 30.12.2006, p. 1, ELI: http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg/2006/1906/oj)." NAMES: Regulation (EC) No 1906/2006; Seventh Framework Programme TENSE: PRESENT as cited — the act itself concerns a programme period stated as 2007-2013 NOTES: Flagged per Research.2 §5b. The citation is made in the present tense inside a 2027 budget, but the instrument governs a programme whose stated period ended in 2013. The Remarks at C0101-045 supply the reconciling language ("and the completion of its predecessor programmes"), but the Legal basis block itself does not mark these acts as historical.
LB-71-03 — Legal basis, act 3 of 7
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 71, "Legal basis" block, third cited act, printed p. 79 QUOTE: "Decision No 1982/2006/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 18 December 2006 concerning the Seventh Framework Programme of the European Community for research, technological development and demonstration activities (2007-2013) (OJ L 412, 30.12.2006, p. 1, ELI: http://data.europa.eu/eli/dec/2006/1982/oj)." NAMES: Decision No 1982/2006/EC; Seventh Framework Programme TENSE: PRESENT as cited — programme period stated as 2007-2013 NOTES: Same §5b flag as LB-71-02.
LB-71-04 — Legal basis, act 4 of 7
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 71, "Legal basis" block, fourth cited act, printed p. 79 QUOTE: "Council Decision 2006/972/EC of 19 December 2006 concerning the specific programme: Ideas implementing the Seventh Framework Programme of the European Community for research, technological development and demonstration activities (2007 to 2013) (OJ L 400, 30.12.2006, p. 243, ELI: http://data.europa.eu/eli/dec/2006/972/oj)." NAMES: Council Decision 2006/972/EC; specific programme: Ideas; Seventh Framework Programme TENSE: PRESENT as cited — programme period stated as 2007 to 2013 NOTES: Decision 2006/972/EC (the "Ideas" specific programme, the FP7 predecessor of the European Research Council) is cited here and only here among the five executive-agency items. The other four cite 2006/971, 2006/973 and 2006/974 instead. The split follows agency mandate as stated in the Reference acts, but the document does not state the connection at this location. Recorded, not adjudicated.
LB-71-05 — Legal basis, act 5 of 7
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 71, "Legal basis" block, fifth cited act, printed p. 79 QUOTE: "Council Decision 2013/743/EU of 3 December 2013 establishing the specific programme implementing Horizon 2020 — the Framework Programme for Research and Innovation (2014-2020) and repealing Decisions 2006/971/EC, 2006/972/EC, 2006/973/EC, 2006/974/EC and 2006/975/EC (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 965, ELI: http://data.europa.eu/eli/dec/2013/743/oj)." NAMES: Council Decision 2013/743/EU; Horizon 2020; Decisions 2006/971/EC, 2006/972/EC, 2006/973/EC, 2006/974/EC, 2006/975/EC TENSE: PRESENT as cited — programme period stated as 2014-2020 NOTES: Internal contradiction on the face of the block, recorded not resolved: this act is cited as legal basis in the same block as Decision 2006/972/EC (LB-71-04), and its own title states that it repeals 2006/972/EC. Both the repealing act and the repealed act appear as live legal bases for the same 2027 budget item. The same pairing occurs in all five executive-agency items, with the respective repealed decisions. Decision 2013/743/EU was itself named in batch 1 at HZ-003 as the act repealed by Council Decision (EU) 2021/764 — so this block cites an act that a third act, already in the corpus, repeals. Three generations of specific-programme decisions are simultaneously present in the corpus as stated legal bases.
LB-71-06 — Legal basis, act 6 of 7
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 71, "Legal basis" block, sixth cited act, printed p. 79 QUOTE: "Regulation (EU) 2015/1017 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 25 June 2015 on the European Fund for Strategic Investments, the European Investment Advisory Hub and the European Investment Project Portal and amending Regulations (EU) No 1291/2013 and (EU) No 1316/2013 — the European Fund for Strategic Investments (OJ L 169, 1.7.2015, p. 1, ELI: http://data.europa.eu/eli/reg/2015/1017/oj)." NAMES: Regulation (EU) 2015/1017; European Fund for Strategic Investments (EFSI); European Investment Advisory Hub; European Investment Project Portal; Regulation (EU) No 1291/2013; Regulation (EU) No 1316/2013 TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Flagged, not resolved. The EFSI Regulation — whose primary subject is investment guarantees, not research — is cited as legal basis for a research executive agency's operating costs. This is the same pattern of cross-Title legal bases already flagged at HZ-004 and HZ-005 in batch 1 (Global Europe and CAP Strategic Plans cited for Horizon Europe). The Remarks blocks do not explain the link in any of the four cases.

LB-71-08 — Legal basis, closing pointer
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 71, "Legal basis" block, final line, printed p. 79 QUOTE: "See Chapter 01 02." NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED — cross-reference only TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Each executive-agency item closes its own Legal basis block with the same deferral to the operational chapter that Article 01 01 01 uses. The item therefore carries both an enumerated list of named acts and a pointer, rather than one or the other.
RA-71-01 — Reference acts
FILE: SEC03.pdf LOCATION: Item 01 01 01 71, "Reference acts" block, printed p. 79 QUOTE: "Commission Decision C(2021) 950 of 12 February 2021 delegating powers to the European Research Council Executive Agency with a view to the performance of tasks linked to the implementation of Union programmes in the field of frontier research comprising, in particular, implementation of appropriations entered in the general budget of the Union." NAMES: Commission Decision C(2021) 950; European Research Council Executive Agency TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: First appearance in the extraction of a "Reference acts" block — a heading distinct from "Legal basis". The document does not state at this location what distinguishes the two categories. Note the citation form: a C(YYYY) number with no OJ reference and no ELI, unlike every act in the Legal basis blocks. C-series Commission decisions are not published in the Official Journal L series; this is a candidate unpublishable or hard-to-locate terminus under Research.2 §4. Not classified here.

Layer 4 — CHAPTER 01 02 figures table (complete)
Printed pp. 88–90. Header: "CHAPTER 01 02 — HORIZON EUROPE / Figures / Title, Chapter, Article, Item / Heading / MFF / 2027 appropriations (Commitments, Payments) / 2026 appropriations (Commitments, Payments) / 2025 out-turn (Commitments, Payments) / Payments (2025/2027)".
This chapter is differentiated: commitments and payments are shown separately. Every item row carries MFF sub-heading code 1.0.11. The printed execution-ratio column is labelled "Payments (2025/2027)" here, against the unlabelled "2025/2027" in Chapter 01 01 — a stated difference in what the ratio measures.
Quote convention for this section: figures are given in printed column order — 2027 CA, 2027 PA, 2026 CA, 2026 PA, 2025 CA out-turn, 2025 PA out-turn, printed ratio.

Batch close
Extracted this batch: 2 corrections to batch 1 (CORR-001, CORR-002); 22 Chapter 01 01 figures-table entries (C0101-006 to C0101-027); 3 chapter-level Remarks entries (C0101-028 to C0101-030); 1 article-level Remarks entry for Article 01 01 01 (C0101-031); 23 item-level Remarks and Assigned revenue entries across Chapter 01 01 (C0101-032 to C0101-054); 15 article- and item-level entries for Articles 01 01 02 and 01 01 03 (C0101-055 to C0101-076); 24 Legal basis entries (LB-71-01 to LB-71-08, LB-72-01/11 grouped, LB-72-02, LB-72-03, LB-72-06 to LB-72-08, LB-72-12, LB-73-01/11 grouped, LB-73-12, LB-74-01/05 grouped, LB-74-06, LB-76-01/11 grouped, LB-76-12); 5 Reference acts entries (RA-71-01 to RA-76-01); 39 Chapter 01 02 figures-table entries (CH0102-001 to CH0102-039). 138 entries total.
Chapter 01 01 is now complete: every figures-table row, every Remarks block, every Assigned revenue block, every Legal basis block and every Reference acts block in the chapter has an entry.
Quotation convention used for repeated acts. Where a legal act's printed text at a later location is word-for-word identical to an act already quoted verbatim at an earlier entry in this batch — same title, same OJ reference, same ELI — the later entry records the location, names the act, and points at the entry holding the full quote rather than repeating it. This departs from strict one-full-quote-per-location. It was done because Items 72, 73 and 76 cite the same eleven acts in the same order with identical printed text, and Item 74 a five-act subset; quoting all 38 in full would add roughly 3,000 words of exact duplication without adding a single checkable string. Every act still carries its own citable location. If the other end of the process wants the full text at every location, the grouped entries (LB-72-01/11, LB-73-01/11, LB-74-01/05, LB-76-01/11) can be expanded mechanically from the entries they point at — flagging this so the choice is visible rather than silent.
Arithmetic checks run and clean (zero residual in every column):
Chapter 01 01: eleven item rows → Article 01 01 01 subtotal, all three years
Chapter 01 01: six item rows → Article 01 01 02 subtotal, all three years
Chapter 01 01: three item rows → Article 01 01 03 subtotal, all three years
Chapter 01 01: three article subtotals → Chapter total, all three years; Chapter total matches the Title 01 summary row (T01-001)
Chapter 01 02: three item rows → Article 01 02 01 subtotal, all six columns
Chapter 01 02: eighteen item rows → Article 01 02 02 subtotal, all six columns (p.m. as nil)
Chapter 01 02: three item rows → Article 01 02 03 subtotal, all six columns
Chapter 01 02: two item rows → Article 01 02 04 subtotal, all six columns
Chapter 01 02: five article subtotals + Article 01 02 05 row → Chapter total, all six columns (p.m. as nil); Chapter total matches the Title 01 summary row (T01-002)
Open discrepancies recorded, not resolved (new in this batch):
Repealed acts cited as live legal bases. All five executive-agency items cite Council Decision 2013/743/EU, whose own printed title states that it repeals Decisions 2006/971/EC, 2006/972/EC, 2006/973/EC, 2006/974/EC and 2006/975/EC — while citing those same repealed decisions as legal bases in the same block. Compounding this, batch 1 recorded at HZ-003 that Council Decision (EU) 2021/764 repeals 2013/743/EU. Three generations of specific-programme decisions therefore sit in the corpus simultaneously as stated legal bases. (LB-71-04, LB-71-05, LB-72-06 to LB-72-08)
Cross-subject legal bases, third and fourth instances. Regulation (EU) 2015/1017 (EFSI — investment guarantees) is cited as legal basis for four of the five research executive agencies, and Decision No 1230/2003/EC (Intelligent Energy — Europe, an energy programme with a stated period of 2003-2006) for four of the five. Neither is explained in the Remarks. This is the same pattern flagged in batch 1 at HZ-004 and HZ-005. (LB-71-06, LB-72-02)
Item 01 01 01 74's legal basis is narrower than its peers — five acts against eleven for Items 72, 73 and 76 — with no stated reason. (LB-74-01/05)
"Other assigned revenue" maps to two different revenue codes within one chapter: 6 0 1 0 in Article 01 01 01, 6 0 1 1 in Article 01 01 02. The label alone is not a reliable key for the expenditure ← revenue link. (C0101-041, C0101-044, C0101-065, C0101-067)
Item 01 01 02 11's Remarks block prints the same "Staff costs comprise…" sentence twice, once before and once after the direct/indirect action indents. The parallel item 01 01 01 11 prints it once. (C0101-063)
Item 01 02 02 31 (European Cybersecurity Industrial, Technology and Research Competence Centre) carries p.m. in all four appropriation columns, 0,— out-turn in both 2025 columns, and a blank execution-ratio cell — a named body with a live budget line and no appropriation in any year shown. (CH0102-012)
Item numbering gap: Article 01 01 01 runs 71, 72, 73, 74, 76 with no item 75. (C0101-015)
Carried forward, still open: the ~EUR 2,3m gap between SEC02's narrative summary figure (EUR 755,0m) and its own itemised expenditure total (EUR 752 774 665); the Title 30 row vs "Of which Reserves" footer difference of 1 807 173 853 (E-019, E-021); the SEC03 Section III total vs SEC(2026) 250 all-sections total (E-020); the Horizon Europe chapter CA difference between SEC03 and SEC(2026) 250 (T01-002, unaffected by this batch — see CH0102-039).
Structural findings for the remaining extraction:
A chapter can carry more than one MFF sub-heading code. Chapter 01 01 uses 1.0.11 (Horizon Europe), 1.0.12 (Euratom) and 1.0.13 (ITER), one per article. The budget-line → MFF-heading link is therefore an article-level property in support-administrative chapters, not a chapter-level one.
The article-level legal-basis deferral targets the matching operational chapter, article by article: 01 01 01 → 01 02, 01 01 02 → 01 03, 01 01 03 → 01 04. Expect the same one-to-one mapping in the other Titles' X 01 chapters. This corrects the batch 1 reading (see CORR-001).
Executive-agency items carry a Legal basis block and the deferral pointer, not one or the other, and they are the only items in Chapter 01 01 with legal-basis blocks of their own.
A second block type exists: "Reference acts." It appears only on executive-agency items, contains exactly one Commission delegation decision each, and uses a C(YYYY) citation form with no OJ reference and no ELI — unlike every act in the Legal basis blocks. Candidate terminus material; the document does not state where these decisions are published.
Revenue codes seen so far: 5 0 4 0 (Proceeds from EURI, Title 5), 6 6 0 0 (EFTA-EEA), 6 0 1 0 (candidate countries / other countries / other assigned revenue), 6 0 1 1 (other assigned revenue, Euratom article only). Expenditure items in Title 01 therefore point back into revenue Titles 5 and 6.
Assigned-revenue composition differs by article. Article 01 01 01's blocks name EFTA-EEA, candidate countries, other countries and (on four items) EURI. Articles 01 01 02 and 01 01 03 name only "Other countries" and "Other assigned revenue". Two items — 01 01 02 01 and 01 01 02 11 — carry no assigned-revenue block at all.
The "In accordance with Articles 21, 22 and 24 of the Financial Regulation…" paragraph is chapter-level boilerplate, appearing verbatim in both Chapter 01 01 and Chapter 01 02. Expect it in every chapter; extract once per chapter with its location, do not treat recurrence as new.
Article rows can carry figures directly. Article 01 02 05 has figures and an MFF code on the article row itself, no items, no subtotal. Table-parsing that assumes figures live only on item and subtotal rows will drop it.
p.m. propagates upward as a token, not a zero. The Article 01 02 99 subtotal reproduces p.m. rather than a figure.
"99" is the completion/legacy article number in Chapter 01 02. Worth checking against other chapters.
The differentiated chapters label the ratio column "Payments (2025/2027)"; the non-differentiated chapters label it "2025/2027". A stated difference in what the printed ratio measures.
Remaining in SEC03, top-down queue (revised):
Chapter 01 02, article and item detail — Remarks, Legal basis, Assigned revenue and Reference acts blocks for Articles 01 02 01 to 01 02 99 and all 28 item rows, from printed p. 91. This is the immediate next batch. Nine Joint Undertakings each have a founding regulation that has not yet surfaced in the corpus; those blocks are the highest-value edge material in the chapter.
Chapters 01 03 (Euratom), 01 04 (ITER), 01 20 (Pilot projects, preparatory actions, prerogatives and other actions). Note that Articles 01 01 02 and 01 01 03 defer their legal basis to 01 03 and 01 04 respectively, so those two chapters close deferrals already recorded.
Titles 02 through 16: full chapter/article/item extraction. Title 08 (Agriculture, 54,9bn CA) and Title 05 (Regional Development, 44,0bn CA) are the two largest and will be the two longest.
Titles 20, 21, 30: administrative expenditure, European Schools and Pensions, Reserves.
Annex 'Staff' to Section III — named at five separate locations in this batch (C0101-045, C0101-047, C0101-049, C0101-051, C0101-053) as the source of the executive agencies' establishment plans. Not yet located in the PDF or scoped.
Annexed Offices (Publications Office, EPSO, PMO, OIB and others), each carrying their own revenue and administrative expenditure titles — a distinct sub-document, not yet scoped.
Revenue Titles 3, 4, 6 (Title 1 and the headline revenue table already done per G.13). Note that Title 5 is now also implicated: revenue code 5 0 4 0 (Proceeds from EURI) is referenced by six expenditure items in Chapter 01 01 alone.
Legal basis block count: batch 1 recorded that "Legal basis" blocks appear 671 times in the document. This batch consumed 8 of them (Articles 01 01 01, 01 01 02 and 01 01 03, and Items 71, 72, 73, 74 and 76), of which 3 are bare pointers. The count is a useful denominator for estimating the remaining work: at roughly the density seen here, the document contains on the order of several thousand individually citable named acts.
Part A — SEC09 extraction (Section IX — European Data Protection Supervisor), complete
Batch ID: SEC09-2026-08-04-A1 Strand: Finances / EU institutional budget — Section IX Governing briefs: Research.2 v2.1 (fixed) + Research.EU v0.1 (fixed) + Research.3 (amendment) Source document: SEC09.pdf — "SECTION IX — EUROPEAN DATA PROTECTION SUPERVISOR", Draft General Budget of the European Union for the financial year 2027. 31 printed pages. Scope: the whole section — Introduction, Revenue (Titles 3, 4), Expenditure (Titles 1, 2, 3, 10), Staff establishment plans S 1 and S 2. Nothing in SEC09 is left unextracted.
CONVENTION CHANGE (applies from this batch onward)
Batches A1 and A2 used one entry per table row, per Remarks block, per cited act. That does not scale: at that granularity SEC09 is ~200 entries and SEC10 ~220, for two documents that are 31 and 38 pages against SEC03's 1,114.
From this batch:
One entry per figures table, with every row quoted verbatim inside the QUOTE field in printed column order. A row remains uniquely locatable by its table plus its own printed text.
Boilerplate formulas quoted once, in a BP-nnn entry, with a complete list of every location where the identical text occurs. No location is dropped.
One entry per substantive Remarks block and per cited act, as before, where the text is not boilerplate.
This is a presentation change, not a sourcing change. Every quote is still verbatim, every location is still recorded, and the batch can be mechanically expanded back to per-row granularity. Flagging it so the choice is visible and reversible.

Layer 1 — Introduction
S09-001 — Cross-section introduction disclaimer
FILE: SEC09.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, opening paragraph (small print), printed p. 1 QUOTE: "The introduction to the budget request has been provided by each Institution together with the statement of estimate for its respective budget section. In line with Article 314§1 of the TFEU, which states that: '[…] each Institution shall […] draw up estimates of its expenditure for the following financial year. The Commission shall consolidate these estimates in a draft budget, which may contain different estimates.', the Commission has exceptionally adjusted the estimates of all Institutions. It is thus possible that figures referred to in the introduction of a specific budget section are different from those integrated into the Draft Budget." NAMES: TFEU (Article 314§1) TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Load-bearing for the whole institutional-sections strand. The document states on its own face that each section's introduction was written by the institution, that the Commission then altered the numbers, and that the introduction may therefore contradict the tables in the same document. This is a stated authorship split inside one publication: narrative by the institution, figures by the Commission. Identical text appears at SEC10.pdf printed p. 1 (see S10-001). See S09-002 and DISC-09-01 for the consequence in this section.
S09-002 — Stated overall 2027 budget
FILE: SEC09.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, printed pp. 1–2 QUOTE: "The proposed overall budget for 2027 amounts to €32.535.000, representing an increase of 5,65%, which should be viewed in context, notably considering the new responsibilities and subsequent need for additional resources arising from the AI Act and the supervision of Large-Scale IT Systems." NAMES: AI Act TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Note the printed thousands separator here is a full stop (€32.535.000), against the space separator used throughout the tables (30 262 189). Two number formats in one document. See DISC-09-01 for the figure discrepancy.
S09-003 — Stated title-level movements
FILE: SEC09.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, printed p. 2 QUOTE: "As regards the EDPS, the proposed budget under Title 1 shows an increase of 2,18%, mainly due to the rebalancing of appropriations under Article 112 which had been overly reduced in 2026. On the other hand, under Title 2, the proposed budget decreases by 5.02% compared to 2026, as a result of the efforts made to better rebalance appropriations." NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED — internal budget-line cross-reference (Article 112) TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: None of the three stated percentages reconciles against the tables in the same document. Table Title 1: 16 431 643 against 16 535 755 is a decrease of 0,63 %, not an increase of 2,18 %. Table Title 2: 4 058 000 against 4 855 724 is a decrease of 16,43 %, not 5,02 %. "Article 112" is the EDPS's own internal numbering for Article 1 1 2 (Other expenditure in connection with staff), whose table figures do move sharply (402 000 against 117 525, +242 %). Recorded, not adjudicated — S09-001 supplies the document's own explanation for why intro and table can differ. Note also the printed decimal separator is inconsistent within one sentence: "2,18%" and "5.02%".
S09-004 — Title 3 movement and its stated cause
FILE: SEC09.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, printed p. 2 QUOTE: "Title 3 of the budget proposal, relating to the activities of the EDPB, shows an increase of 17,25%. The growth in Title 3 is mainly due to the European Commission's unilateral termination of the SLA with the DGT, which had granted the EDPB access to free translation services and consequently results in a substantial financial impact." NAMES: EDPB; European Commission; DGT (Directorate-General for Translation) TENSE: PRESENT / PAST NOTES: A named service-level agreement between two EU bodies, stated as terminated, with a stated budgetary consequence. The SLA itself is not cited by number or date and is a candidate unpublishable terminus under Research.2 §4. Table Title 3: 9 772 546 against 9 403 857 is +3,92 %, not 17,25 %. The translation line specifically (Item 3 0 4 1) moves 692 277 → 882 746, +27,5 %.
S09-005 — Court of Justice ruling and EDPB litigation exposure
FILE: SEC09.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, printed p. 1 QUOTE: "With its decision of 10 February 2026 (case C-97/23 P) the Court of Justice of the EU set aside a ruling of the General Court and ruled that a binding decision of the EDPB is an act open to challenge before the Courts of the European Union. Seven cases, corresponding to over €2.5bn of fines and settling key legal questions, will have to be defended on substance." NAMES: Court of Justice of the European Union (case C-97/23 P, decision of 10 February 2026); General Court; EDPB TENSE: PAST as to the ruling; FUTURE as to the cases NOTES: A named judgment with a date and case number cited as a driver of a budget line. Post-dates the corpus's other material. The €2.5bn figure is stated as an aggregate of fines under challenge, not as a budget figure, and appears nowhere in the tables.
S09-006 — Regulation 2025/2518 and its application date
FILE: SEC09.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, printed p. 1 QUOTE: "In addition, the EDPB must prepare for Regulation 2025/2518 laying down additional procedural rules relating to the enforcement of Regulation (EU) 2016/679, which will be applicable as of 2 April 2027. New legal duties stemming from this regulation require setting up new workflows, procedures and IT tools for collaboration on cross-border cases; additional resources for the EDPB's IT sector are needed to deliver and maintain these tools." NAMES: Regulation 2025/2518; Regulation (EU) 2016/679 (GDPR) TENSE: FUTURE ("will be applicable as of 2 April 2027") NOTES: Regulation 2025/2518 is cited without OJ reference and without ELI, unlike every act cited in the section's Legal basis blocks. A named act with a stated future application date driving a current appropriation. This is the clearest forward-dated dependency in the section: a 2027 IT appropriation stated as caused by an act applicable from 2 April 2027.
S09-007 — AI Act roles
FILE: SEC09.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, printed p. 1 QUOTE: "Additionally, since the entry into force of the AI Act, the EDPS is the Market Surveillance Authority, Notifying Authority and Notified Body for all EU institutions falling within the scope of this regulation." NAMES: AI Act TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Three distinct statutory roles asserted for one body. The AI Act is named by short title only — no number, no OJ reference, no ELI, anywhere in SEC09.
S09-008 — Large-Scale IT Systems and cost recovery
FILE: SEC09.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, printed p. 1 QUOTE: "Recognising this challenge, recent regulations include provisions stating that costs incurred by the EDPS in carrying out these newly assigned tasks must be covered by the general budget of the EU." NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED — "recent regulations" unnamed TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Unnamed terminus. A class of acts is asserted to contain a cost-recovery obligation, but no act is named, numbered or cited. Under Research.2 the edge does not exist: no document is identified. The preceding sentences name the driver as Large-Scale IT Systems "expected to become operational between 2026 and 2029" in the area of freedom, security and justice (AFSJ), also without naming the systems or the acts establishing them.
S09-009 — Printed text defect in the introduction
FILE: SEC09.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, printed p. 1, end of the EDPB litigation paragraph QUOTE: "It is therefore essential that the EDPB is reinforced, to continue supporting the application of the GDPR. the" NAMES: GDPR TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: The paragraph terminates with a stranded lower-case "the" after the full stop. Quoted verbatim per Research.2 §6, not tidied. Recorded because it is evidence the introduction text is institution-supplied and not copy-edited into the consolidated document — consistent with the authorship split stated at S09-001.

S09-036 — Title 3 chapter table
FILE: SEC09.pdf LOCATION: TITLE 3 — EUROPEAN DATA PROTECTION BOARD, "Figures" table, printed p. 20 QUOTE: "3 0 EXPENDITURE IN CONNECTION WITH THE OPERATION OF THE EUROPEAN DATA PROTECTION BOARD 9 772 546 9 403 857 8 163 525,90 / Title 3 — Total 9 772 546 9 403 857 8 163 525,90" NAMES: European Data Protection Board TENSE: N/A — figures table NOTES: Single chapter. Note the collision in numbering: "Title 3" and "Chapter 3 0" exist on both the revenue and the expenditure side of this section with entirely different content — revenue Title 3 is Administrative revenue, expenditure Title 3 is the EDPB; revenue Chapter 3 0 is Revenue from staff, expenditure Chapter 3 0 is EDPB operation. Any index keyed on "3 0" without a revenue/expenditure discriminator will merge two unrelated lines. Same collision exists in SEC10.

NAMES: Regulation (EU, Euratom) 2024/2509 (the Financial Regulation) TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: The only place in SEC09 where the Financial Regulation is cited in full. Note the OJ reference format: "OJ L, 2024/2509, 26.9.2024" — the post-2023 OJ numbering with no L-number and no page, unlike every older act in the section. Chapter 10 0 carries no Remarks block, unlike its SEC10 counterpart which does (S10-032).

The EDPB plan has no post above AD 12 and no AST/SC posts at all. Batch close — SEC09
Coverage: complete. Every table, Remarks block, Legal basis block and establishment plan in Section IX has an entry. 9 introduction entries, 9 boilerplate entries covering 62 individual locations, 7 revenue table entries, 10 revenue Remarks/Legal basis entries, 9 expenditure table entries, 10 expenditure Remarks entries, 14 expenditure Legal basis entries, 2 establishment plan entries. 70 entries covering the whole section.
Arithmetic checks run, all clean (zero residual): revenue summary; Title 3 chapters; Chapter 3 0 articles and items; expenditure summary; Title 1 chapters; Chapter 1 0 articles and items; Chapter 1 1 articles and items; Chapter 2 0; Chapter 3 0 (expenditure) articles and items; both establishment plans. Printed execution ratios spot-checked against their own figures at Articles 1 0 0, 1 0 1, 1 1 2 and Chapter 1 0 — all consistent.
DISC-09-01 — Introduction total against expenditure table. The introduction states €32.535.000 and an increase of 5,65 % (S09-002). The expenditure table totals 30 262 189 against a 2026 figure of 30 795 336 — a decrease of 1,73 %. Gap: 2 272 811. The introduction's title-level percentages fail the same way: Title 1 stated +2,18 %, table −0,63 %; Title 2 stated −5,02 %, table −16,43 %; Title 3 stated +17,25 %, table +3,92 %. The document supplies its own explanation at S09-001 — the institution wrote the introduction, the Commission then adjusted the figures. Recorded, not adjudicated.
DISC-09-02 — Candidate explanation for the open SEC02 discrepancy. The G.13 queue carries an unresolved ~EUR 2,3m gap between SEC02's narrative summary (EUR 755,0m) and its own itemised total (752 774 665). SEC09 shows the identical shape at 2 272 811, and SEC10 at 27 256 945 (DISC-10-01), both in documents carrying the S09-001 disclaimer explicitly. This is a candidate common cause, not a resolution: the disclaimer does not name the SEC02 figure, does not state the direction or size of any adjustment, and no document in the corpus states that the SEC02 gap arises this way. Belongs in Part B as a soft connection at most.
Open items, recorded not resolved:
Three variants of the Article 21 assigned-revenue formula within four printed pages: 21(3) ×7, 21(2) ×1 (Article 3 3 1), unqualified 21 ×1 (Article 3 3 8). (BP-09-01 to BP-09-03)
Every one of the 32 expenditure-side assigned-revenue notes is p.m. The expenditure→revenue link is declared throughout SEC09 and quantified nowhere. (BP-09-04)
Regulation No 260/68 carries two different printed name forms across sections — "Regulation (EEC, Euratom, ECSC) No 260/68 of the Council" in SEC09, "Council Regulation (EEC, Euratom, ECSC) No 260/68" in SEC10 — same OJ reference, same ELI. (S09-020, S10-015)
Item 3 0 1 2 has a Legal basis in SEC10 and none in SEC09. Same budget line, same subject. (S09-024, S10-018)
Item 3 0 1 1's pension-rights citation differs between sections: SEC09 specifies Article 11(2) and (3), SEC10 says Articles 4 and 11. (S09-023, S10-017)
Mission-expenses Annex VII citation has three printed forms in two sections: "Articles 11, 12 and 13", "Articles 11 to 13", and SEC10's Article-71-less variant. (S09-054, S10-025)
The EDPB's own buildings line describes offices provided to the EDPS. (S09-059)
Item 3 0 4 1 cites a translation agreement in the present tense that the introduction states has been unilaterally terminated. (S09-004, S09-060)
Four unnamed instruments cited as sole legal bases — two EP accommodation agreements, two "agreement … with the institution providing the service". No dates, numbers, OJ references or ELIs. Candidate unpublishable termini. (S09-058, S09-059, S09-060)
"Recent regulations" cited as imposing a cost-recovery obligation, with no act named. Under Research.2 no edge exists. (S09-008)
Regulation 2025/2518 and the AI Act are cited without OJ reference or ELI, unlike every act in the Legal basis blocks. (S09-006, S09-007)
Title 4's heading names default interest and fines; the Title contains neither. (S09-016)
Both establishment plans are flat year on year (78 and 27) against an introduction arguing for reinforcement on four separate grounds. (S09-070, S09-071)
Item 1 0 0 1 drops from 74 687 to p.m. while the introduction states the next EDPS selection procedure is ongoing. (S09-041)
Structural findings:
The introduction of every institutional section is institution-authored and Commission-adjusted, and the document says so. Intro figures are not reliable against tables anywhere in Sections I–X. This should be assumed for SEC01, SEC02 and SEC04–SEC08 before they are extracted.
The MFF code series differs by section. SEC03 uses 1.0.1x; SEC09 uses 7.2.9xx (7.2.911, .912, .921, .922, .923, .931, .932, .933, .934, .941, .951, .961, .962, .974, .981, .982, .983). Ten distinct codes in a 31-page section.
Three structural variants of where figures live, all present in SEC09: on item rows (normal), on an article row with no items or subtotal (Article 2 0 0), and directly under a chapter heading with no articles or items (Chapters 10 0, 10 1).
Rows whose 2025 out-turn is 0,— or whose current figure is p.m. carry a blank execution-ratio cell, not a zero. Confirms the SEC03 finding at a second section.
"Title 3" and "Chapter 3 0" exist on both the revenue and expenditure side of the same section with unrelated content. Any index must carry a revenue/expenditure discriminator.
One institution's section can contain another body's complete administrative structure as a single chapter — the EDPB occupies Title 3 with its own buildings, staff, other-staff, staff-expenditure and operations articles, plus its own establishment plan (S 2).
The Staff Regulations and Conditions of Employment are the most-cited and least-precisely-cited instruments in the section — no OJ reference, no ELI, in every occurrence across both sections.
Part A — SEC10 extraction (Section X — European External Action Service), complete
Batch ID: SEC10-2026-08-04-A1 Strand: Finances / EU institutional budget — Section X Governing briefs: Research.2 v2.1 (fixed) + Research.EU v0.1 (fixed) + Research.3 (amendment) Source document: SEC10.pdf — "SECTION X — EUROPEAN EXTERNAL ACTION SERVICE", Draft General Budget of the European Union for the financial year 2027. 38 printed pages. Scope: the whole section — Introduction, Revenue (Titles 3, 4, 6), Expenditure (Titles 1, 2, 3, 10), Staff establishment plan S 1. Nothing in SEC10 is left unextracted. Convention: as set out in the SEC09 batch (one entry per figures table with all rows quoted; boilerplate quoted once with a full location list; one entry per substantive block or cited act).

Layer 1 — Introduction
S10-001 — Cross-section introduction disclaimer
FILE: SEC10.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, opening paragraph (small print), printed p. 1 QUOTE: "The introduction to the budget request has been provided by each Institution together with the statement of estimate for its respective budget section. In line with Article 314§1 of the TFEU, which states that: '[…] each Institution shall […] draw up estimates of its expenditure for the following financial year. The Commission shall consolidate these estimates in a draft budget, which may contain different estimates.', the Commission has exceptionally adjusted the estimates of all Institutions. It is thus possible that figures referred to in the introduction of a specific budget section are different from those integrated into the Draft Budget." NAMES: TFEU (Article 314§1) TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Verbatim identical to S09-001 apart from one character: SEC09 prints a space before the closing quotation mark ("different estimates. '"), SEC10 does not ("different estimates.'"). Two occurrences confirm this is standing boilerplate across the institutional sections rather than a one-off. See DISC-10-01.
S10-002 — Stated overall 2027 budget
FILE: SEC10.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, "1 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY DB2027", printed p. 1 QUOTE: "The EEAS draft budget for 2027 amounts to EUR 992.7 million, representing an increase of 4.7% compared to the 2026 budget. This increase primarily reflects the need to reinforce the EU's security and defence capacity in response to the evolving geopolitical environment, as well as to address structural underbudgeting in certain operational expenditure lines, particularly in the context of expected renewed high inflation worldwide." NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: The expenditure table totals 965 443 055 against 947 728 110 — an increase of 1,87 %, not 4,7 %, and a level 27 256 945 below the stated figure. See DISC-10-01. Note SEC10 uses full-stop decimals ("992.7", "4.7%") where SEC09's introduction mixes comma and full stop.
S10-003 — Establishment date and delegation network
FILE: SEC10.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, printed p. 1 QUOTE: "Since its establishment in 2011, the European External Action Service (EEAS) has progressively strengthened its role as the diplomatic service of the European Union, implementing the EU's Common Foreign and Security Policy … Through its network of 145 EU Delegations and offices worldwide, the EEAS contributes to a wide range of policy areas, including security and defence under the Common Security and Defence Policy, crisis response, climate diplomacy, mediation and peacebuilding, development cooperation, humanitarian action, and the promotion of human rights." S10-004 — Security and Defence package, post counts

Batch close — SEC10
Coverage: complete. Every table, Remarks block, Legal basis block and the establishment plan in Section X has an entry. 35 entries covering the whole section, of which 8 boilerplate entries cover 82 individual locations.
Arithmetic checks run, all clean (zero residual): revenue summary; Title 3 chapters; Chapter 3 0 articles and items; Chapters 3 1, 3 2, 3 3; Titles 4 and 6 with their chapters; expenditure summary (all six columns); Title 1 and Chapters 1 1, 1 2, 1 3, 1 4, 1 5; Title 2 and Chapters 2 0, 2 1, 2 2 (all six columns); Title 3 and Chapter 3 0; establishment plan both years.
DISC-10-01 — Introduction total against expenditure table. Introduction states EUR 992.7 million and +4.7 % (S10-002). Table totals 965 443 055 against 947 728 110, +1,87 %. Gap: 27 256 945. Same shape as DISC-09-01 and the open SEC02 item, and the document supplies the same self-explanation at S10-001.
DISC-10-02 — Revenue estimate against realised revenue. 2027 administrative revenue is estimated at 67 844 000 against a 2025 out-turn of 374 946 558,94, an 81,9 % drop, because Chapters 3 1, 3 2 and 3 3 are estimated at p.m. despite realising 311 470 410,82 between them in 2025. p.m. is here doing duty over very large realised flows, not over dormant lines. Recorded, not adjudicated.
