Open items, recorded not resolved:
The MFF sub-heading codes in SEC10 contain an unresolved placeholder character — 7.2.X11, 7.2.X12, 7.2.X31 … 7.2.X9PPPA, 7.1.2X. SEC09's equivalents are fully resolved digits. The key is not derivable from this document. Highest-priority machine-readability issue found so far. (S10-022)
Four printed variants of the Article 21 assigned-revenue sentence within SEC10, and a fifth-and-sixth across the two sections: SEC09 says "on the lines which bore" and cites 21(3); SEC10 mostly says "in the headings which bore" and cites 21(2) and (3) — but uses SEC09's wording twice. (BP-10-01, BP-10-04, BP-10-05)
Eight unnamed or under-cited instruments carrying material appropriations: HR/VP Decision of 4 February 2014 (no number, no OJ, no ELI — sole basis for 22 372 532); HR/VP Decision on mission rules (no number, no date); Decision No 111/2007 (number and authority, no OJ, no ELI); the VP/HR letter of 13 September 2016 to the European Parliament; "Rules governing designation, remuneration and other financial conditions adopted by the EEAS" (sole distinguishing basis for 166 213 000); the "Mandate and Service agreement signed with the Commission"; "Gender Action Plan III"; and "the related Staff Working Document" — the last naming an instrument only by its relation to another unnamed one. (BP-10-08, S10-027, S10-030, S10-031, S10-033)
Council Directive 89/391/EEC (1989 workers' health and safety) cited as legal basis for a buildings fitting-out line. Same cross-subject pattern as SEC03 HZ-004/005 and LB-71-06. (S10-032)
The section is printed in differentiated format but commitments equal payments to the cent in every row except one (Item 2 2 5 0, the pilot project, which is p.m.). (S10-021)
Title 3 carries 60,5 % of the section for 145 delegations with no geographic breakdown of any kind. (S10-024, S10-003)
The EEAS receives translation free from the Council Secretariat and the Commission (Item 2 2 2 0, p.m. in all years) while SEC09 states the EDPB has just lost the equivalent arrangement. Neither document connects the two. (S10-032, S09-004)
Cross-section citation inconsistencies for the same budget lines, carried from the SEC09 close: Regulation No 260/68's name form; Item 3 0 1 2's Legal basis present in SEC10 and absent in SEC09; Item 3 0 1 1's pension-rights articles; three printed forms of the Annex VII mission citation; two printed headings for Item 3 2 0 2; SEC09's Title 4 lacking the Chapter 4 1 its own heading implies. (S10-015 to S10-018, S10-031, S10-012, S10-014)
Structural findings (new or confirmed at a second section):
Sections differ in budget-line granularity for identical statutory content. SEC09 puts twelve staff-cost components in one item's Remarks; SEC10 spreads the same entitlements across five items. Any cross-section comparison at item level is unsafe; only Title and Chapter totals are comparable.
Five tiers of citation precision now observable: (a) full title + OJ + ELI; (b) title + article, no OJ, no ELI (Staff Regulations, Protocol); (c) number + adopting authority, no OJ, no ELI (Decision No 111/2007); (d) adopting authority + date only (HR/VP Decision of 4 February 2014); (e) description only, no number, no date, no authority (the EP accommodation agreements, the EEAS's own Rules, Gender Action Plan III). Tiers (c) to (e) are all candidate unpublishable termini and are common in institutional sections in a way they are not in SEC03.
The closed quantified loop at Article 3 3 2 ↔ Item 3 0 0 5 is the model case for what the financial-model goal is looking for: both ends named, the amount stated identically at both, and the five downstream sibling items enumerated. Worth using as the template when scanning SEC04–SEC08.
Confirmed at a second and third section: article rows can carry figures with no items or subtotal (Article 1 4 0, Article 1 2 2, Article 2 2 4, and SEC09's Article 2 0 0); chapters can carry figures with no articles at all (Title 10 in both); blank rather than zero ratio cells where the out-turn is 0,— or the appropriation p.m.; and the revenue/expenditure "Title 3 / Chapter 3 0" numbering collision.
Establishment plans are flat in all three plans extracted so far (EDPS 78, EDPB 27, EEAS 1 750), with real internal grade movement in the EEAS plan. Introductions in both sections argue for reinforcement; the plans do not show it, and in SEC10's case that is consistent because the stated new posts are SNEs and contract agents.
Remaining in the institutional-sections strand: SEC04 (Court of Justice), SEC05 (Court of Auditors), SEC06 (European Economic and Social Committee), SEC07 (Committee of the Regions), SEC08 (European Ombudsman). None held. On the evidence of SEC09 and SEC10 each should be 25–40 pages and a single session at this convention. SEC01 and SEC02 are held at headline level only and need the same full treatment.
SEC07 — Section VII, European Committee of the Regions — Part A batch
File: SEC07.pdf (35 printed pages, Draft Budget 2027) Governing: Research.2.md v2.1 (fixed) + Research.EU.md v0.1 (fixed) + Research.3.md (amendment) Convention: as from SEC09 — one entry per figures table with all rows quoted verbatim inside it; boilerplate quoted once with a complete location list; one entry per substantive block or cited act. Session: 2026-08-04. Queue item 1 (institutional sections, none previously held).

Layer 1 — Introduction
S07-001 — Cross-section introduction disclaimer
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, opening paragraph (small print), printed p. 1 QUOTE: "The introduction to the budget request has been provided by each Institution together with the statement of estimate for its respective budget section. In line with Article 314§1 of the TFEU, which states that: '[…] each Institution shall […] draw up estimates of its expenditure for the following financial year. The Commission shall consolidate these estimates in a draft budget, which may contain different estimates.', the Commission has exceptionally adjusted the estimates of all Institutions. It is thus possible that figures referred to in the introduction of a specific budget section are different from those integrated into the Draft Budget." NAMES: TFEU (Article 314§1) TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Third occurrence of this identical text in the corpus, after SEC09.pdf p. 1 (S09-001) and SEC10.pdf p. 1 (S10-001). Character-for-character identical at all three locations. The stated authorship split — narrative by the institution, figures by the Commission — is now confirmed as a standing feature of every institutional section rather than a two-document coincidence. See DISC-07-01 and DISC-07-02 for the consequence in this section, which appears in two independent forms here.
S07-002 — Institutional self-description and Member count
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, "1 BACKGROUND", printed p. 1 QUOTE: "The European Committee of the Regions (CoR) is a political assembly comprising 329 elected representatives and 329 alternates from local and regional authorities across all EU countries. Most CoR Members are affiliated with one of six political groups: EPP, PES, Renew Europe, ECR, EA, and The Greens & Progressives. The CoR's main mission is to shape EU policies and influence decision-making from a local and regional perspective. Notably, these authorities are responsible for implementing around 70% of European law." NAMES: European Committee of the Regions (CoR); EPP; PES; Renew Europe; ECR; EA; The Greens & Progressives TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: The 70 % figure is stated without a source and without a citation to any instrument. Under the Research.2 §6 tiers this is tier (e) — an unattributed quantified claim, a candidate unpublishable terminus. The 329/329 Member count is load-bearing for S07-003, which compares the CoR's budget to the EESC's on the basis of equal Member numbers.
S07-003 — Stated comparison with the EESC
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, "1 BACKGROUND", printed p. 1 QUOTE: "Compounding this, the CoR operates with 36% less budget than the European Economic and Social Committee (EESC) under the current MFF, despite having the exact same number of Members. For an institution that the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union places on equal footing with the EESC, and whose Members are democratically elected representatives of local and regional authorities, this funding gap is simply unacceptable." NAMES: European Economic and Social Committee (EESC); Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union; MFF TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: A quantified cross-section comparison stated in SEC07 about SEC06 (EESC), which is not held. The 36 % is not sourced to any table, in this document or by reference. This is the first stated inter-institutional budget comparison in the corpus and it points at an unheld section — it should be checked against SEC06 when SEC06 is extracted, not before. Recorded as stated, not adjudicated. See also S07-018, which gives a second, differently-scoped CoR/EESC figure pair for Title 2 joint services.
S07-004 — Stated 2027 budget request total and post count
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, "2 2027 BUDGET REQUEST", printed p. 3 QUOTE: "After in-depth discussions and reviews at both administrative and political levels, the CoR is requesting a total DB 2027 of € 142,600,414, representing a € 7.3 million (5.4%) increase over the 2026 voted budget and 498 posts in the establishment plan ( 1 new specialised cybersecurity post )." NAMES: NO PUBLICATION NAMED TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Both stated figures contradict the tables in the same document. The expenditure summary totals 138 750 785 (S07-030); the establishment plan grand total is 497 for 2027, identical to 2026 (S07-062). See DISC-07-01 and DISC-07-02. Note also the number format: the introduction prints comma thousands separators (€ 142,600,414) and decimal-point millions (€ 7.3 million), while every table in the same document uses space separators (138 750 785) and comma decimals (129 813 772,21). Same three-format split recorded at SEC09 (S09-002), which used full stops instead.
S07-005 — Stated budgetary strategic guidelines and their adopting body
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, "2 2027 BUDGET REQUEST", printed p. 2 QUOTE: "Following approval by the CoR's Bureau on 9 December 2025, the main elements of the CoR's budgetary strategic guidelines are as follows: (i) Ensure at least the "status quo" of political activities, taking into account formal inflation and salary indexation estimates provided to all Institutions by the European Commission; (ii) Reinforce funding for CoR Members and communication activities of CoR political groups; (iii) Expand the Local Dialogues' programme ; (iv) Enhance Members' visibility and increase the CoR's institutional and political impact; (v) Allocate necessary resources to support to the joint EC-CoR's EU Local Councillors' Network ; (vi) Strengthen communication activities for CoR Members ; (vii) Reinforce CoR cybersecurity with 1 AD5 post; (viii) Improve the CoR's cloud development and data safety ; (ix) Provide funding for mandatory SLAs for new corporate document management and finance tools (Ares, SUMMA, EdiT)." NAMES: CoR Bureau; European Commission; EU Local Councillors' Network; Ares; SUMMA; EdiT TENSE: PRESENT (guidelines) / PAST (approval, 9 December 2025) NOTES: A named decision by a named body on a named date, with no document reference, no register number and no citation — tier (d) under Research.2 §6, a candidate unpublishable terminus. Ares, SUMMA and EdiT are named as corporate tools without any instrument establishing them; three further unnamed termini. Guideline (vii) states 1 AD 5 post; the establishment plan shows AD 5 unchanged at 5 permanent posts in both years (S07-062).
S07-006 — Stated composition of the increase
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, "2 2027 BUDGET REQUEST", printed p. 3 QUOTE: "This budget increase covers, among others, the following new projects: — Funding for CoR Members and communication activities of CoR political groups € 0.123 million — Local Dialogues' programme € 0.1 million — EU Local Councillor's Network € 0.5 million — Members' visibility to increase the CoR's institutional and political impact € 0.3 million — Communication activities for CoR Members € 0.165 million — 1 new AD5 permanent post (cybersecurity) as of July 2027 € 0.1 million — Cloud development and data safety € 1 million — Funding for mandatory SLAs for new corporate document management and finance tools (Ares, SUMMA, EdiT) € 0.2 million — Security and surveillance of buildings € 0.74 million" NAMES: EU Local Councillor's Network; Ares; SUMMA; EdiT TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Nine stated amounts. The document does not map any of them to a budget line, and the amounts are stated at a precision (0.1 million) that the tables do not carry. Two are checkable against table movements and both fail: "Security and surveillance of buildings € 0.74 million" against Item 2 0 2 6, which moves 2 247 481 → 2 308 876, a rise of 61 395 (S07-046); "EU Local Councillor's Network € 0.5 million" against Item 2 6 0 3, which moves 50 000 → 100 000, a rise of 50 000 (S07-056). Recorded, not resolved — the S07-001 disclaimer covers the direction of travel but does not state these specific adjustments.
S07-007 — Stated share of Heading 7 and of the overall budget
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, "2 2027 BUDGET REQUEST", printed p. 3 QUOTE: "It is important to highlight that the CoR has significantly increased its political activities in recent years, directly impacting its budget. While all CoR expenditure falls under Heading 7 (administrative spending), similar to the European Parliament, a substantial share of the budget supports non-administrative activities, particularly costs associated with Members and their political work. Despite this heightened activity, the CoR's 2027 budget accounts for only about 1% of total Heading 7 expenditure and just 0.07% of the overall EU budget." NAMES: Heading 7; European Parliament TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: The document states that all CoR expenditure sits under MFF Heading 7. Every MFF code in the section's tables begins 7.2 (DISC-07-03), which is consistent, though the document does not connect the two statements. The 1 % and 0.07 % are unsourced. Cross-check available but not performed here: 0.07 % of SEC(2026) 250's EUR 199 905,1 million headline would be ~EUR 140m, in the region of the introduction's own 142 600 414 rather than the table's 138 750 785 — noted as an observation about which figure the percentage was computed from, not as a resolution of DISC-07-01.
S07-008 — Stated execution rate and absence of reserves
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: INTRODUCTION, "2 2027 BUDGET REQUEST", printed p. 3 QUOTE: "The CoR has consistently achieved excellent annual budget outturns in recent years. Over the past three years, it has maintained an impressive 99.9% budget execution rate, the highest in its history. This near-complete utilisation of the budget leaves no internal reserves, demonstrating strong financial management but also highlighting the need for additional resources, a point reinforced by the EP 2023 and 2024 Discharge reports." NAMES: EP 2023 Discharge report; EP 2024 Discharge report TENSE: PRESENT / PAST NOTES: Two named documents cited by year and issuing institution only — no title, no number, no OJ reference, no ELI. Tier (d) under Research.2 §6: identifiable in principle, not citable from this location. These are the first European Parliament discharge reports named anywhere in the corpus and they are recurrently published, so they are candidate nodes. The 99.9 % claim is not reconcilable with this document's own printed 2025/2027 execution-ratio column, which ranges from 38,26 % (Item 2 1 0 3) to 848,31 % (Item 2 0 0 0) — but that column has a different denominator (2027 appropriations, not 2025), so the two are not the same measure and no contradiction is asserted.

Layer 2 — Boilerplate
BP-07-01 — Article 21(3) assigned-revenue formula, revenue side
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: 8 locations, listed below QUOTE: "In accordance with Article 21(3) of the Financial Regulation, this revenue is to be considered as assigned revenue and gives rise to the entry of additional appropriations in the headings which bore the initial expenditure giving rise to the corresponding revenue." NAMES: Financial Regulation (Article 21(3)) TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Identical text at: Article 3 1 0 Remarks (printed p. 6); Article 3 1 2 Remarks (p. 6); Item 3 2 0 2 Remarks (p. 7); Article 3 2 1 Remarks (p. 7); Article 3 2 2 Remarks (p. 7); Article 3 3 0 Remarks (p. 8); Article 3 3 3 Remarks (p. 8); Article 3 3 8 Remarks (p. 8). Eight locations. The Financial Regulation is named only as "the Financial Regulation" at every one of them — its citation number, Regulation (EU, Euratom) 2024/2509, appears exactly once in this section, at Article 4 1 9 (S07-024), and is never restated. Same naming pattern as SEC09 and SEC10; same pattern as SEC03 HZ-007.
BP-07-02 — Article 21(2) variant
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: Article 3 3 1 Remarks, printed p. 8 QUOTE: "In accordance with Article 21(2) of the Financial Regulation, this revenue is to be considered as assigned revenue and gives rise to the entry of additional appropriations in the headings which bore the initial expenditure giving rise to the corresponding revenue." NAMES: Financial Regulation (Article 21(2)) TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: One location. Identical to BP-07-01 except the sub-paragraph: 21(2) rather than 21(3), at the article for income from foundations, subsidies, gifts and bequests. SEC09 carries the same 21(2) variant at the same budget line, Article 3 3 1 (BP-09-02). Two sections, same article number, same sub-paragraph switch. SEC09 additionally carried an unqualified "Article 21" variant at Article 3 3 8 (BP-09-03); SEC07 does not — its 3 3 8 uses 21(3). So SEC07 has two variants where SEC09 had three, and the 3 3 1 → 21(2) mapping is the one both sections share.
BP-07-03 — Quantified assigned-revenue estimate, expenditure side
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: 5 locations, listed below QUOTE: "The amount of assigned revenue in accordance with Article 21(3) of the Financial Regulation is estimated at EUR [amount]." NAMES: Financial Regulation (Article 21(3)) TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Five locations, each with its own figure: Item 1 0 0 4 — EUR 4 000 (printed p. 12); Item 1 4 0 2 — EUR 13 000 (p. 16); Item 1 4 2 0 — EUR 2 500 (p. 17); Article 2 1 6 — EUR 3 000 (p. 26); Article 2 3 2 — EUR 2 500 (p. 27). Total stated: EUR 25 000. This is the material contrast with SEC09. SEC09 declared 32 expenditure-side assigned-revenue links and quantified none of them — all 32 were p.m. (BP-09-04). SEC07 declares five and quantifies all five. SEC10 quantified 16 totalling EUR 333 636 500. So the corpus now holds three institutional sections with three different practices for the same field. None of the five SEC07 amounts names the revenue article that receives them, so unlike the SEC10 Article 3 3 2 ↔ Item 3 0 0 5 case (S10 batch close, finding 2) these do not close a quantified loop — the expenditure end is quantified and the revenue end is p.m. throughout Chapters 3 1, 3 2 and 3 3 (S07-021 to S07-024).
BP-07-04 — Provisional-appropriation transfer formula
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: 4 locations, listed below QUOTE: "This appropriation is purely provisional and may be used only after its transfer to other headings in accordance with the Financial Regulation." NAMES: Financial Regulation TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: At Article 1 4 9 (printed p. 18); Item 2 0 0 9 (p. 24); and, with "purely" absent — "This appropriation is provisional and may be used only after its transfer to other headings in accordance with the Financial Regulation." — at Article 1 2 9 (p. 14). Chapter 10 0 (p. 33) carries a fourth variant: "This appropriation is purely provisional and may be used only after transfer to other budget chapters in accordance with the Financial Regulation." — "budget chapters" rather than "headings", and no "its". Four locations, three wordings, one mechanism. Recorded because the wording differences are in the operative words (headings vs chapters) and the document does not state whether they mean different things.
BP-07-05 — Bare Staff Regulations / CEOS citation
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: 6 locations, listed below QUOTE: "Staff Regulations of Officials of the European Union. Conditions of Employment of Other Servants of the European Union." NAMES: Staff Regulations of Officials of the European Union; Conditions of Employment of Other Servants of the European Union TENSE: N/A — legal basis citation NOTES: Both instruments cited by name only, with no OJ reference and no ELI, as the complete Legal basis block at: Item 1 2 0 0 (printed p. 13); Item 1 2 0 4 (p. 13); Item 1 4 0 0 (p. 15); Item 1 4 0 8 (p. 17); Article 1 6 2, following an article-specific line (p. 19); Article 1 4 9, following an article-specific line (p. 18). Item 1 2 0 2 cites the Staff Regulations with Article 56 and Annex VI, then CEOS bare. The Staff Regulations are the single most-cited instrument in this section and are never once given with a citation number or ELI anywhere in it. Tier (c) under Research.2 §6 — named precisely, locatable, but not citable from any location in this document. Identical practice in SEC09 and SEC10.

S07-018 — Item 3 0 0 0 Legal basis
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: Item 3 0 0 0 — Tax on remunerations, "Legal basis" block, printed p. 4 QUOTE: "Protocol on the privileges and immunities of the European Union, and in particular Article 12 thereof. NAMES: Protocol on the privileges and immunities of the European Union; Regulation (EEC, Euratom, ECSC) No 260/68 TENSE: N/A — legal basis citation NOTES: Name-form check against the open SEC09/SEC10 item. The batch close for SEC09 recorded that Regulation No 260/68 carries two different printed name forms across sections — "Regulation (EEC, Euratom, ECSC) No 260/68 of the Council" in SEC09 (S09-020) against "Council Regulation (EEC, Euratom, ECSC) No 260/68" in SEC10 (S10-015), same OJ reference, same ELI. SEC07 prints the SEC09 form. Two sections to one; no document states which is canonical. The Protocol is cited by name and article with no number, no OJ reference and no ELI — tier (c), and it is a primary-law instrument, so this is the least citable form in the section attached to the most authoritative source.
S07-019 — Items 3 0 0 1, 3 0 1 0, 3 0 1 1 and 3 0 1 2 Legal basis blocks
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: Item 3 0 0 1 (printed p. 5); Item 3 0 1 0 (p. 5); Item 3 0 1 1 (p. 5); Item 3 0 1 2 (p. 5), "Legal basis" blocks QUOTE: "Staff Regulations of Officials of the European Union, and in particular Article 66a thereof." and "Staff Regulations of Officials of the European Union, and in particular Article 83(2) thereof." and "Staff Regulations of Officials of the European Union, and in particular Article 11(2) and Articles 17 and 48 of Annex VIII thereto." and "Staff Regulations of Officials of the European Union, and in particular Article 40(3) and Article 83(2) thereof. Conditions of Employment of Other Servants of the European Union, and in particular Articles 41 and 43 thereof." NAMES: Staff Regulations of Officials of the European Union; Conditions of Employment of Other Servants of the European Union TENSE: N/A — legal basis citation NOTES: Two open cross-section items close here, both against SEC10 and not SEC09. (i) Item 3 0 1 2 has a Legal basis block in SEC07; the SEC09 batch close recorded that SEC10 had one and SEC09 had none at this same budget line (S09-024, S10-018). SEC07 sides with SEC10 — two sections carry it, one does not. (ii) Item 3 0 1 1's pension-rights citation was recorded as differing between sections: SEC09 specifies Article 11(2) and (3); SEC10 says Articles 4 and 11. SEC07 gives a third form — Article 11(2) and Articles 17 and 48 of Annex VIII. Three sections, three different citations, same budget line, same subject. No document reconciles them. This is now the sharpest citation-divergence case in the corpus and it is not a naming variant but a substantive difference in which provisions are invoked.

S07-045 — Title 2 joint-services remark
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: TITLE 2, "Remarks" block immediately below the Title table, printed p. 21 QUOTE: "In 2026, the initial appropriations for the two committees' joint services, under Title 2, amounted to EUR 35 942 482 for the European Economic and Social Committee and EUR 26 257 635 for the European Committee of the Regions." SEC07 states a 2026 figure for SEC06 (EESC) inside SEC07's own document. Two observations, neither adjudicated: (i) the stated CoR figure for 2026 joint services (26 257 635) is not the same as this document's own 2026 Title 2 total (31 436 433) — the difference is 5 178 798, and the document does not state the relationship between "joint services under Title 2" and "Title 2"; (ii) the ratio of the two stated figures (26 257 635 / 35 942 482 = 73,1 %, i.e. the CoR at 26,9 % less) does not match the 36 % gap asserted in the introduction at S07-003, but the two statements have different scopes — one is Title 2 joint services in 2026, the other is the whole budget under the current MFF — so this is a scope difference and not necessarily a contradiction. Verifiable against SEC06 when SEC06 is held; not before. This is a candidate hard edge SEC07 → SEC06, the strongest inter-institutional link found so far in the institutional-sections strand, because it states a named figure for a named other institution rather than a comparison.

S07-055 — Item 1 6 3 8 Remarks — childcare facilities operated by Union institutions
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: Item 1 6 3 8 — Early Childhood Centre and approved day nurseries, "Remarks" block, printed p. 21 QUOTE: "This appropriation is intended to cover the contributions of the European Committee of the Regions to the costs of nursery centres and other day care and after-school centres operated by or approved by the institutions of the Union, and any other expenditure generated for childcare facility purposes." NAMES: European Committee of the Regions TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: A stated contribution of 700 000 for 2027 flowing to facilities "operated by or approved by the institutions of the Union", with no institution named and no receiving budget line identified. A quantified expenditure with an unnamed inter-institutional counterparty — the same shape as Item 3 2 0 2 on the revenue side (S07-014), and the two are not connected by any statement in the document.
S07-056 — Item 2 6 0 3 Remarks — EU Local Councillors Network provenance
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: Item 2 6 0 3 — EU Councillors, "Remarks" block, printed p. 31 QUOTE: "This appropriation is intended for the European Committee of the Regions' contribution to the EU Local Councillors Network. This was created at the end of 2024 through the merger of the 'Building Europe with Local Entities (BELE)' pilot project run by the European Commission and the Committee's network of regional and local councillors. This 'EU' first branded network is the most ambitious joint project of the Union institutions for reaching out to elected representatives at regional and local levels. It is featured in the mission letters of all the European Commissioners as well as the political guidelines for the second term of office of Commission President von der Leyen. This initiative has its origins in a European Parliament pilot project and the European Parliament will continue to be represented in the Steering Committee." NAMES: European Committee of the Regions; EU Local Councillors Network; Building Europe with Local Entities (BELE); European Commission; European Parliament; Commission President von der Leyen TENSE: PAST (creation, origins) / PRESENT (representation) NOTES: Densest naming in the section — six named entities, four named documents-or-instruments, none citable. "the mission letters of all the European Commissioners" and "the political guidelines for the second term of office of Commission President von der Leyen" are named documents with no date, number or reference; the BELE pilot project and the European Parliament pilot project are named programmes with no legal basis given. Five candidate unpublishable termini in one Remarks block, tier (d) throughout. Note the item name in the table is "EU Councillors" and the name in the Remarks is "EU Local Councillors Network" — and the introduction (S07-005, S07-006) uses a third form, "EU Local Councillor's Network" and "EU Local Councillors' Network". Four printed forms of one name inside one document.
S07-057 — Item 2 6 2 4 Remarks — Historical Archives of the European Union
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: Item 2 6 2 4 — Expenditure on archive resources, "Remarks" block, second paragraph, printed p. 33 QUOTE: "It also covers the expenditure for the transfer of the historical archives of the Committee to the Historical Archives of the European Union (managed by the European University Institute in Florence)." NAMES: Historical Archives of the European Union; European University Institute TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: A stated flow from a Union institution to a body outside the Union budget structure entirely, with the managing entity named. No instrument establishing the arrangement is cited, no amount is separated out from the item total of 180 993. Recorded as a named external terminus.
S07-058 — Item 2 5 4 4 Remarks — RegHub and ERASMUS for local and regional representatives
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: Item 2 5 4 4 — Support to networks and fora, "Remarks" block, printed p. 29 QUOTE: "This appropriation is intended to cover expenses of a new nature related to support to networks and fora, such as: — lump-sum contributions to the RegHub network (both as an incentive for experts to give their input and for the outreach activities of these networks to local stakeholders), — vouchers for local and regional politicians in the Union to attend training on Union affairs as part of the 'ERASMUS for local and regional representatives' project." NAMES: RegHub network; 'ERASMUS for local and regional representatives' project TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: Two named programmes with no legal basis, no establishing act, and no amount — the item is p.m. in all three columns. Both are candidate unpublishable termini. The ERASMUS naming is notable: the project borrows the name of a major Union programme (Erasmus+) without citing it or stating any relationship to it.
S07-059 — Item 2 1 0 0 Remarks — service level agreements with Union institutions
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: Item 2 1 0 0 — Purchase, servicing and maintenance of equipment and software, and related work, "Remarks" block, second paragraph, printed p. 25 QUOTE: "This appropriation is also intended to cover the costs associated with service level agreements signed with Union institutions (e.g. for the use of information systems, notably with the Commission for Sysper, EU Learn, ABAC, and other related applications) and re-invoicing of other services (notably for IT procurement)." NAMES: European Commission; Sysper; EU Learn; ABAC TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: A second stated purchase from the Commission (SEC03), after S07-053, this time naming three specific Commission information systems. Sysper, EU Learn and ABAC are named applications with no establishing instrument; ABAC is the Commission's accounting system and therefore load-bearing for the budget-execution chain, and it is named here without any reference. The service level agreements themselves are named as a class of signed instruments with none identified. Together with the introduction's Ares, SUMMA and EdiT (S07-005), the section names seven Commission or corporate IT systems and cites none of them.
S07-060 — Item 2 6 0 1 Remarks — Conference on the Future of Europe
FILE: SEC07.pdf LOCATION: Item 2 6 0 1 — Permanent dialogue mechanism, "Remarks" block, first paragraph, printed p. 31 QUOTE: "This nomenclature responds to new realities of organising events after the COVID-19 pandemic and to the Conference on the Future of Europe. In particular, there is the call for a permanent dialogue mechanism with Union citizens at all levels, which includes regional and local levels." NAMES: Conference on the Future of Europe TENSE: PRESENT NOTES: The budget line's existence is stated to respond to a named process with no document cited — no final report, no date, no reference. The Conference on the Future of Europe produced published output that is not named here. Tier (d), candidate unpublishable, and one where a budget line's justification rests entirely on the uncited document.

Batch close — SEC07
Coverage: complete. Every table, Remarks block, Legal basis block and the establishment plan in Section VII has an entry. 8 introduction entries, 5 boilerplate entries covering 24 individual locations, 8 revenue table entries, 8 revenue Remarks/Legal basis entries, 13 expenditure table entries, 18 expenditure Remarks/Legal basis entries, 1 establishment plan entry. 61 entries covering the whole 35-page section.
Arithmetic checks run, all clean (zero residual): revenue summary; Title 3 chapters; Chapter 3 0 articles and items; Chapters 3 1, 3 2, 3 3; Title 4 chapters; Chapters 4 0, 4 1, 4 2; expenditure summary; Title 1 chapters; Chapters 1 0, 1 2, 1 4, 1 6 articles and items; Title 2 chapters; Chapters 2 0, 2 1, 2 3, 2 5, 2 6 articles and items; Title 10; establishment plan (all four columns, all three subtotals, both totals). Printed execution ratios spot-checked against their own figures at Items 1 0 0 0, 1 0 0 4, 1 2 0 0, 2 0 0 0, 2 1 0 0, 2 6 0 3, Article 2 3 1 and Chapters 1 2 and 2 3 — all consistent. Ratio convention confirmed as 2025 out-turn ÷ 2027 appropriations, same as SEC09 and SEC10.
DISC-07-01 — Introduction total against expenditure table
The introduction states a DB 2027 of € 142,600,414 and an increase of € 7.3 million (5.4%) over the 2026 voted budget (S07-004). The expenditure table totals 138 750 785 against a 2026 figure of 135 305 895 — an increase of 3 444 890, or 2,55 % (S07-030). Gap on the total: 3 849 629. The document supplies its own explanation at S07-001 — the institution wrote the introduction, the Commission then adjusted the figures. Recorded, not adjudicated.
DISC-07-02 — Introduction post count against establishment plan
The introduction states "498 posts in the establishment plan ( 1 new specialised cybersecurity post )" (S07-004) and the strategic guidelines state "Reinforce CoR cybersecurity with 1 AD5 post" (S07-005). The establishment plan grand total is 497 for 2027 and 497 for 2026, with AD 5 unchanged at 5 permanent posts in both years (S07-062). Gap: 1 post, and the stated grade shows no movement.
This is a materially different kind of discrepancy from DISC-07-01, DISC-09-01 and DISC-10-01, and it is worth separating. Those three are all monetary gaps between an institution-authored narrative figure and a Commission-adjusted table. This one is a headcount gap. The S07-001 disclaimer speaks only of the Commission adjusting "the estimates" — a financial term — and says nothing about establishment plans. Whether the disclaimer covers post counts is not stated anywhere in the document. Recorded as open.
DISC-07-03 — Third distinct MFF-code practice; candidate structure for the SEC10 placeholder
This is the highest-value finding in the batch and it bears directly on the corpus's highest-priority machine-readability issue (G.14 queue, finding 3).
SEC07 uses 21 distinct MFF sub-heading codes, all beginning 7.2, all with a resolved third digit of 7, plus one alphabetic form:
7.2.711, 7.2.712, 7.2.723, 7.2.731, 7.2.732, 7.2.733, 7.2.734, 7.2.741, 7.2.751, 7.2.752, 7.2.753, 7.2.761, 7.2.762, 7.2.771, 7.2.772, 7.2.773, 7.2.774, 7.2.781, 7.2.782, 7.2.783, 7.2.79SPEC
Set against the two sections already held:
SEC09 (Section IX, EDPS) — third digit 9 throughout: 7.2.911, 7.2.912, 7.2.921, 7.2.922, 7.2.923, 7.2.931 … 7.2.983. Plus 7.1.1, 7.1.2, 7.1.3.
SEC10 (Section X, EEAS) — third position X throughout: 7.2.X11, 7.2.X12, 7.2.X31 … 7.2.X83, plus 7.2.X9PPPA and 7.1.2X.
SEC07 (Section VII, CoR) — third digit 7 throughout, plus 7.2.79SPEC.
Three observations, all extraction facts:
The third character tracks the section number in each of the three sections held: Section VII → 7, Section IX → 9, Section X → X.
The final two characters form a vocabulary shared across sections — 11 (remuneration), 12 (other staff), 31/32/33/34, 51/52/53, 61/62, 71/72/73/74, 81/82/83 all appear in at least two of the three sections at comparable budget lines.
The alphabetic-suffix form is not unique to SEC10. SEC07 prints 7.2.79SPEC — a resolved third digit followed by "9" and a four-letter suffix — at four locations (Items 2 5 4 1, 2 5 4 4, 2 6 0 3 and Article 2 6 4). SEC10's 7.2.X9PPPA has the same shape: third character, then "9", then an alphabetic suffix.
What this does and does not settle. Observation 3 settles something real: SEC10's alphabetic suffix is not a corruption, a redaction or an extraction artifact, because a structurally identical form appears in a fully resolved section. The "X9…" slot is a genuine code family.
Observation 1 is a candidate reading only, and it is inference, not extraction. No document in the corpus states that the third character encodes the section number, and the reading rests on three data points. If it holds, SEC10's X is the Roman numeral for its own section number — Section X — and is not a placeholder at all, which would dissolve the problem rather than solve it. This belongs in Part B as a soft connection and must not be recorded as a resolution. Under Research.2 §5a, the useful output here is that a plausible-looking reading now exists and can be tested cheaply: SEC04, SEC05, SEC06 and SEC08 will each confirm or break it in a single glance at any chapter table. SEC06 (EESC) should be the test case, since S07-045 gives an independent reason to hold it next.
Note also that SEC07 carries no 7.1.x codes at all, where SEC09 has 7.1.1, 7.1.2 and 7.1.3. Consistent with the CoR having no pensions or European Schools lines, but the document does not say so.
Open items, recorded not resolved
Item 3 0 1 1 pension-rights citation now differs three ways across three sections — SEC09: Article 11(2) and (3). SEC10: Articles 4 and 11. SEC07: Article 11(2) and Articles 17 and 48 of Annex VIII. Same budget line, same subject, three different sets of invoked provisions, no reconciliation stated. (S07-019, S09-023, S10-017)
Item 3 0 1 2 Legal basis — present in SEC10 and SEC07, absent in SEC09. Two to one; the SEC09 batch close recorded this as open on one data point and it remains open with three. (S07-019, S09-024, S10-018)
Regulation No 260/68 name form — SEC07 prints the SEC09 form ("Regulation (EEC, Euratom, ECSC) No 260/68 of the Council"), against SEC10's inverted form. Two to one, same OJ reference, same ELI, no canonical form stated. A parallel within-document case appears at Regulation No 3518/85 (S07-039), where the Remarks and the Legal basis block on the same page print two different forms of one instrument name.
The expenditure→revenue assigned-revenue link is quantified at the expenditure end and p.m. at the revenue end. Five expenditure items state amounts totalling EUR 25 000 (BP-07-03); every revenue article that could receive them is p.m. for 2026 and 2027 (S07-013 to S07-015). No SEC07 expenditure item names the revenue article that feeds it, so unlike SEC10's Article 3 3 2 ↔ Item 3 0 0 5 case this does not close a loop. Three sections, three practices: SEC09 declares 32 links and quantifies none; SEC10 quantifies 16 totalling EUR 333 636 500 and names the counterpart article at least once; SEC07 quantifies 5 totalling EUR 25 000 and names no counterpart.
The 6,0 % standard abatement (S07-034) is applied to 58,8 % of the section's expenditure with no instrument named, no pre-abatement figure given, and no statement of where the rate is set. This is the single largest unstated arithmetic operation in the section and it stands between the establishment plan and the remuneration appropriations.
Introduction project amounts do not reconcile to table movements. Two of the nine stated new-project amounts are checkable and both fail (S07-006). Covered in direction by the S07-001 disclaimer, not in detail.
Three quantified inter-institutional flows with unnamed or unlocated counterparts — Item 3 2 0 2 receives over EUR 3,2m from "other Union institutions, bodies, offices and agencies", none named (S07-014); Item 1 4 0 2 purchases interpretation from the Commission and the European Parliament with no split and no counterpart revenue line identified (S07-053); Item 1 6 3 8 contributes 700 000 to childcare facilities "operated by or approved by the institutions of the Union", none named (S07-055).
S07-045 is a candidate hard edge SEC07 → SEC06. SEC07 states a named 2026 Title 2 joint-services figure for the EESC (EUR 35 942 482) alongside its own (EUR 26 257 635). This is the first stated quantified cross-section figure in the institutional-sections strand. It is checkable only against SEC06, which is unheld. Its relationship to the introduction's 36 % claim (S07-003) is a scope difference, not necessarily a contradiction.
Under-cited instruments in SEC07 (tiers c–e)
Fifteen, comparable to SEC10's eight over a similar page count: Protocol on the privileges and immunities of the European Union (two name forms, no reference); Staff Regulations of Officials of the European Union (no reference, cited at 14 locations); Conditions of Employment of Other Servants of the European Union (no reference); "the Financial Regulation" (11 locations without its number, one with); CoR Bureau decision of 9 December 2025; European Committee of the Regions decision on working regime; missions guide of the European Committee of the Regions; EP 2023 and 2024 Discharge reports; mission letters of all the European Commissioners; political guidelines for the second term of office of Commission President von der Leyen; BELE pilot project; the European Parliament pilot project behind the EU Local Councillors Network; RegHub network; 'ERASMUS for local and regional representatives' project; Conference on the Future of Europe. Plus seven named IT systems with no instrument (Sysper, EU Learn, ABAC, Ares, SUMMA, EdiT, and the Committee's own information systems) and the Translation Centre for the Bodies of the European Union.
Fully cited with OJ reference and ELI: four instruments only — Regulation (EEC, Euratom, ECSC) No 260/68, Regulation (EU, Euratom) 2024/2509, Council Regulation (ECSC, EEC, Euratom) No 3518/85, and the pair Decision 2002/620/EC and Decision 2002/621/EC.
Presentation rule observed
Field format (FILE / LOCATION / QUOTE / NAMES / TENSE / NOTES), full parameter values where present, no adjudication. All quotes verbatim. All locations recorded. Boilerplate entries mechanically expandable back to per-location granularity. Derived percentages and differences in NOTES fields are computed, not quoted from the source, and are the softest content in this batch.
